\displaystyle 2d_{y}\bar{\delta}_{N}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|X\|_{\infty}/2)]\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}/2)

\displaystyle+2d_{y}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{P}[\|f_{0}(X)\|_{\infty}^{4}]\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|X\|_{\infty})]\right\}^{1/2}\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}/2)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{1}(\bar{\delta}_{N}^{2}+1)\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}/2),

where
c_{1}
is a constant which depends only on
d_{y},\varsigma,\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|X\|_{\infty})]
and
\mathbb{E}_{P}[\|f_{0}(X)\|_{\infty}^{4}]
. On the other hand, we first notice that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{\|f(X)-f_{0}(X)\|_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N})\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{\|f(X)-f_{0}(X)\|_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}(\|f_{0}(X)\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N})\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N})\right\}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{\|f(X)-f_{0}(X)\|_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}(\|f_{0}(X)\|_{\infty}>\iota_{N})\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{\|f(X)-f_{0}(X)\|_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}(\|f_{0}(X)\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N})\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N})\right\}

\displaystyle+2d_{y}\bar{\delta}_{N}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\mathds{1}(\|f_{0}(X)\|_{\infty}>\iota_{N})\right]+2d_{y}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left[\|f_{0}(X)\|_{\infty}^{2}\mathds{1}(\|f_{0}(X)\|_{\infty}>\iota_{N})\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{\|f(X)-f_{0}(X)\|_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}(\|f_{0}(X)\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N})\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N})\right\}

\displaystyle+2d_{y}\bar{\delta}_{N}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|f_{0}(X)\|_{\infty}/2)]\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}/2)

\displaystyle+\frac{32}{\varsigma^{2}}d_{y}\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|f_{0}(X)\|_{\infty})]\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}/2)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{\|f(X)-f_{0}(X)\|_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}(\|f_{0}(X)\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N})\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N})\right\}+c_{2}(\bar{\delta}_{N}^{2}+1)\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}/2),

where
c_{2}
is a constant which depends only on
d_{y},\varsigma
and
\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|f_{0}(X)\|_{\infty})]
. Then, we focus on
\{x:\|x\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N}\}=[-\iota_{N},\iota_{N}]^{d_{x}}
. Fix an arbitrary
j\in\{1,\dots,d_{y}\}
. Let
h_{j}(x)=f_{0,(j)}(2\iota_{N}x-\iota_{N}\mathrm{1}_{d_{x}})
for
x\in[0,1]^{d_{x}}
. Lemma
A.2
demonstrates that for any
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, there exists a function
h_{j}^{*}
implemented by a ReLU network with depth
L^{*}=21(\lfloor\beta_{f}\rfloor+1)^{2}S_{1}\lceil\log_{2}(8S_{1})\rceil+2d_{x}
, width
M^{*}=38(\lfloor\beta_{f}\rfloor+1)^{2}d_{x}^{\lfloor\beta_{f}\rfloor+1}S_{2}\lceil\log_{2}(8S_{2})\rceil
, such that

|h_{j}^{*}(x)-h_{j}(x)|\leq 18c(\iota_{N}^{m}+1)(\lfloor\beta_{f}\rfloor+1)^{2}d_{x}^{\lfloor\beta_{f}\rfloor+(\beta_{f}\vee 1)/2}(S_{1}S_{2})^{-2\beta_{f}/d_{x}},

for all
x\in[0,1]^{d_{x}}\backslash\Omega([0,1]^{d_{x}},K,\Delta)
. Here,

\Omega([0,1]^{d_{x}},K,\Delta)=\bigcup_{i=1}^{d_{x}}\left\{x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{d_{x}})^{\top}:x_{i}\in\bigcup_{k=1}^{K-1}(k/K-\Delta,k/K)\right\},

where
K=\lceil(S_{1}S_{2})^{2/d_{x}}\rceil
and
\Delta
is an arbitrary scalar in
(0,1/(3K)]
. Let
h_{j}^{\dagger}(x)=h_{j}^{*}((x+\iota_{N}\mathrm{1}_{d_{x}})/(2\iota_{N}))
for
x\in[-\iota_{N},\iota_{N}]^{d_{x}}
. We obtain that

|h_{j}^{\dagger}(x)-f_{0,(j)}(x)|\leq 18c(\iota_{N}^{m}+1)(\lfloor\beta_{f}\rfloor+1)^{2}d_{x}^{\lfloor\beta_{f}\rfloor+(\beta_{f}\vee 1)/2}(S_{1}S_{2})^{-2\beta_{f}/d_{x}},

for all
x\in[-\iota_{N},\iota_{N}]^{d_{x}}\backslash\Omega^{\dagger}
, where
\Omega^{\dagger}=\{x:(x+\iota_{N}\mathrm{1}_{d_{x}})/(2\iota_{N})\in\Omega([0,1]^{d_{x}},K,\Delta)\}
. Furthermore, note that

h_{j}^{\dagger}(x)=h_{j}^{*}\left(\frac{x+\iota_{N}\mathrm{1}_{d_{x}}}{2\iota_{N}}\right)=h_{j}^{*}\left(\mathrm{relu}\left(\frac{x+\iota_{N}\mathrm{1}_{d_{x}}}{2\iota_{N}}\right)-\mathrm{relu}\left(-\frac{x+\iota_{N}\mathrm{1}_{d_{x}}}{2\iota_{N}}\right)\right),

which is implemented by a neural network with ReLU activations, depth
L^{\dagger}=L^{*}+1
, and width
M^{\dagger}=M^{*}
. In addition, let

h_{j}^{\ddagger}(x)=\begin{cases}\bar{\delta}_{N},&h_{j}^{\dagger}(x)>\bar{\delta}_{N},\\
h_{j}^{\dagger}(x),&\underline{\delta}_{N}\leq h_{j}^{\dagger}(x)\leq\bar{\delta}_{N},\\
\underline{\delta}_{N},&h_{j}^{\dagger}(x)<\underline{\delta}_{N}.\end{cases}

A straightforward calculation shows that

h_{j}^{\ddagger}(x)=\mathrm{relu}(-\mathrm{relu}(-h_{j}^{\dagger}(x)+\bar{\delta}_{N})+\bar{\delta}_{N})-\mathrm{relu}(-\mathrm{relu}(h_{j}^{\dagger}(x)-\underline{\delta}_{N})-\underline{\delta}_{N}),

indicating that
h_{j}^{\ddagger}(x)
can be implemented by a ReLU network with depth
L^{\ddagger}=L^{*}+3
and width
M^{\ddagger}=M^{*}
. Due to the arbitrariness of
\Delta
, when
\bar{\delta}_{N}\geq\iota_{N}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{[h_{j}^{\ddagger}(X)-f_{0,(j)}(X)]^{2}\mathds{1}(\|f_{0}(X)\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N})\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left[18c(\iota_{N}^{m}+1)(\lfloor\beta_{f}\rfloor+1)^{2}d_{x}^{\lfloor\beta_{f}\rfloor+(\beta_{f}\vee 1)/2}(S_{1}S_{2})^{-2\beta_{f}/d_{x}}\right]^{2}.

Let
f^{\ddagger}(x)=(h_{1}^{\ddagger}(x),\dots,h_{d_{y}}^{\ddagger}(x))^{\top}
. It is straightforward to verify that
f^{\ddagger}
can be implemented by a ReLU network in
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN}}^{d_{y}}
with depth
L=L^{*}+3
and width
M=d_{y}M^{*}
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN}}^{d_{y}}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{\|f(X)-f_{0}(X)\|_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}(\|f_{0}(X)\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N})\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{\|f^{\ddagger}(X)-f_{0}(X)\|_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}(\|f_{0}(X)\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N})\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N})\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{d_{y}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{[h_{j}^{\ddagger}(X)-f_{0,(j)}(X)]^{2}\mathds{1}(\|f_{0}(X)\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N})\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle d_{y}\left[18c(\iota_{N}^{m}+1)(\lfloor\beta_{f}\rfloor+1)^{2}d_{x}^{\lfloor\beta_{f}\rfloor+(\beta_{f}\vee 1)/2}(S_{1}S_{2})^{-2\beta_{f}/d_{x}}\right]^{2},

provided that
\bar{\delta}_{N}\geq\iota_{N}
. Recall that
\bar{\delta}_{N}=(\log N)^{1+\kappa}
, and set
\iota_{N}
to
(2\varsigma^{-1}\log N)\vee 1
. We conclude that for sufficiently large
N
satisfying
\bar{\delta}_{N}\geq\iota_{N}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN}}^{d_{y}}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\|f(X)-f_{0}(X)\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN}}^{d_{y}}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{\|f(X)-f_{0}(X)\|_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}(\|f_{0}(X)\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N})\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N})\right\}

\displaystyle+(c_{1}+c_{2})(\bar{\delta}_{N}^{2}+1)\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}/2)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{3}\left\{\left[(\lfloor\beta_{f}\rfloor+1)^{2}d_{x}^{\lfloor\beta_{f}\rfloor+(\beta_{f}\vee 1)/2}(S_{1}S_{2})^{-2\beta_{f}/d_{x}}(\log N)^{m}\right]^{2}+\frac{(\log N)^{2+2\kappa}}{N}\right\},

where
c_{3}
is a constant not depending on
S_{1},S_{2}
and
N
.
∎

To commence, we notice that
\|f_{0}(X^{s})\|_{\infty}
is sub-exponentially distributed provided that
\|Y^{s}\|_{\infty}
is a sub-exponential random variable (consider Jensen’s inequality). Then, Lemma
B.7
, Lemma
B.8
and Lemma
B.9
indicate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\hat{f}^{s}_{N}(X^{s})-f_{0}(X^{s})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{1}SL\log S(\log N)^{5+4\kappa}}{N}

\displaystyle+c_{2}\left\{\left[(\lfloor\beta_{f}\rfloor+1)^{2}d_{x}^{\lfloor\beta_{f}\rfloor+(\beta_{f}\vee 1)/2}(S_{1}S_{2})^{-2\beta_{f}/d_{x}}(\log N)^{m}\right]^{2}+\frac{(\log N)^{2+2\kappa}}{N}\right\},

where
c_{1},c_{2}
are constants not depending on
S,L,S_{1},S_{2}
and
N
, and
S_{1},S_{2}
satisfy the conditions that the network depth
L=21(\lfloor\beta_{f}\rfloor+1)^{2}S_{1}\lceil\log_{2}(8S_{1})\rceil+2d_{x}+3
, network width
M=38d_{y}(\lfloor\beta_{f}\rfloor+1)^{2}d_{x}^{\lfloor\beta_{f}\rfloor+1}S_{2}\lceil\log_{2}(8S_{2})\rceil
, for sufficiently large
N
and
N\geq\mathrm{Pdim}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN}})
. Therefore, by letting
S_{1}=\mathcal{O}(N^{d_{x}/(2d_{x}+4\beta_{f})})
and
S_{2}=\mathcal{O}(1)
, we obtain

M=\mathcal{O}(1),\quad L=\mathcal{O}\left(N^{\frac{d_{x}}{2d_{x}+4\beta_{f}}}\log N\right),\quad S=\mathcal{O}(M^{2}L)=\mathcal{O}\left(N^{\frac{d_{x}}{2d_{x}+4\beta_{f}}}\log N\right),

yielding

\mathbb{E}\|\hat{f}^{s}_{N}(X^{s})-f_{0}(X^{s})\|_{2}^{2}\leq c_{3}N^{-\frac{2\beta_{f}}{d_{x}+2\beta_{f}}}(\log N)^{(8+4\kappa)\vee(2m)},

where
c_{3}
is a constant not depending on
N
, for
N\geq 2
. Furthermore, observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\hat{f}^{s}_{N}(X^{t})-f_{0}(X^{t})\|_{2}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\left\{\|\hat{f}^{s}_{N}(X^{s})-f_{0}(X^{s})\|_{2}^{2}\cdot r_{0}(X^{s})\right\}.

As
\|\hat{f}^{s}_{N}(X^{s})\|_{\infty}
is bounded by
\bar{\delta}_{N}=(\log N)^{1+\kappa}
,
\|f_{0}(X^{s})\|_{\infty}
is a sub-exponential random variable (hence it possesses a finite fourth moment), and
r_{0}(X^{s})
is presumed to be sub-exponentially distributed, by Corollary 4.2, we conclude that

\mathbb{E}\|\hat{f}^{s}_{N}(X^{t})-f_{0}(X^{t})\|_{2}^{2}\leq c_{4}N^{-\frac{2\beta_{f}}{d_{x}+2\beta_{f}}}(\log N)^{(8+4\kappa)\vee(2m)+1}+\frac{c_{5}d_{y}[(\log N)^{2+2\kappa}+1]}{N},

for
N\geq 2
, where
c_{4},c_{5}
are constants which do not depend on
N
. This completes the proof.
∎

B.8
Proof of Lemma 5.2

In this subsection, we abbreviate
\mathbb{E}[h(X^{s},Y^{s})]
to
\mathbb{E}_{P}[h(X,Y)]
for any
(X^{s},Y^{s})
-integrable function
h
, whenever the expectation is taken with respect to
(X^{s},Y^{s})
.

We first observe that

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{t}

\displaystyle=\int\mathbb{E}\left[W_{2}^{2}(\rho_{0,x}\|\hat{\rho}^{s}_{x})\right]q(x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int\mathbb{E}\left[W_{2}^{2}(\rho_{0,x}\|\hat{\rho}^{s}_{x})\right]p(x)r_{0}(x)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[W_{2}^{2}(\rho_{0,X^{s}}\|\hat{\rho}^{s}_{X^{s}})r_{0}(X^{s})\right].

For any
\iota_{N}>0
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{t}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[W_{2}^{2}(\rho_{0,X^{s}}\|\hat{\rho}^{s}_{X^{s}})r_{0}(X^{s})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[W_{2}^{2}(\rho_{0,X^{s}}\|\hat{\rho}^{s}_{X^{s}})r_{0}(X^{s})\mathds{1}(r_{0}(X^{s})\leq\iota_{N})\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[W_{2}^{2}(\rho_{0,X^{s}}\|\hat{\rho}^{s}_{X^{s}})r_{0}(X^{s})\mathds{1}(r_{0}(X^{s})>\iota_{N})\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\iota_{N}\mathcal{E}^{s}+\mathbb{E}\left[W_{2}^{2}(\rho_{0,X^{s}}\|\hat{\rho}^{s}_{X^{s}})r_{0}(X^{s})\mathds{1}(r_{0}(X^{s})>\iota_{N})\right].

Fix any
x\in\mathcal{X}^{s}
. Consider the following two ODEs

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}Z_{\tau}

\displaystyle=v_{0}(x,Z_{\tau},\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau,\quad Z_{0}\sim N(0,I_{d_{y}}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\hat{Z}_{\tau}

\displaystyle=\hat{v}^{s}_{N}(x,\hat{Z}_{\tau},\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau,\quad\hat{Z}_{0}\sim N(0,I_{d_{y}}).

We denote the particles at time
\tau\in[0,1]
as
Z_{\tau}(x,z)
and
\hat{Z}_{\tau}(x,z)
given
Z_{0}=z
and
\hat{Z}_{0}=z
, respectively. Then, it follows that

\displaystyle W_{2}^{2}(\rho_{0,x}\|\hat{\rho}^{s}_{x})\leq

\displaystyle\int\left\|Z_{1}(x,z)-\hat{Z}_{1}(x,z)\right\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\lambda(z)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\int\left\|Z_{1}(x,z)\right\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\lambda(z)\mathrm{d}z+2\int\left\|\hat{Z}_{1}(x,z)\right\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\lambda(z)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}\left(\|Y^{s}\|_{2}^{2}\big|X^{s}=x\right)+2\int\left\|\hat{Z}_{1}(x,z)\right\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\lambda(z)\mathrm{d}z,

where
\lambda(\cdot)
represents the density function of
d_{y}
-dimensional standard Gaussian distribution. Furthermore, define

\displaystyle H_{1}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\|Y^{s}\|_{2}^{2}\big|X^{s}=x\right),

\displaystyle H_{2}(x,\tau)

\displaystyle=\int\left\|\hat{Z}_{\tau}(x,z)\right\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\lambda(z)\mathrm{d}z,\quad\text{for }\tau\in[0,1].

Observe that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}\tau}H_{2}(x,\tau)

\displaystyle=2\int\left\langle\hat{v}^{s}_{N}(x,\hat{Z}_{\tau}(x,z),\tau),\hat{Z}_{\tau}(x,z)\right\rangle\cdot\lambda(z)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\leq\int\left\|\hat{v}^{s}_{N}(x,\hat{Z}_{\tau}(x,z),\tau)\right\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\lambda(z)\mathrm{d}z+\int\left\|\hat{Z}_{\tau}(x,z)\right\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\lambda(z)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\leq d_{y}\max(\bar{\delta}^{2},\underline{\delta}^{2})+H_{2}(x,\tau).

Let
\delta^{*}=\max(\bar{\delta}^{2},\underline{\delta}^{2})
. By Lemma
A.7
, we obtain

\int\left\|\hat{Z}_{1}(x,z)\right\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\lambda(z)\mathrm{d}z=H_{2}(x,1)\leq d_{y}e(\delta^{*}+1).

Hence, we conclude that
W_{2}^{2}(\rho_{0,x}\|\hat{\rho}^{s}_{x})\leq 2H_{1}(x)+2d_{y}e(\delta^{*}+1)
for any
x\in\mathcal{X}^{s}
. As a consequence, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{t}\leq

\displaystyle\iota_{N}\mathcal{E}^{s}+\mathbb{E}\left[W_{2}^{2}(\rho_{0,X^{s}}\|\hat{\rho}^{s}_{X^{s}})r_{0}(X^{s})\mathds{1}(r_{0}(X^{s})>\iota_{N})\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\iota_{N}\mathcal{E}^{s}+2\mathbb{E}_{P}[H_{1}(X)r_{0}(X)\mathds{1}(r_{0}(X)>\iota_{N})]+2d_{y}e(\delta^{*}+1)\mathbb{E}_{P}[r_{0}(X^{s})\mathds{1}(r_{0}(X^{s})>\iota_{N})]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\iota_{N}\mathcal{E}^{s}+\frac{8}{\varsigma}\mathbb{E}_{P}[H_{1}(X)\exp(\varsigma r_{0}(X)/2)]\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}/4)

\displaystyle+\frac{4d_{y}e}{\varsigma}(\delta^{*}+1)\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma r_{0}(X))]\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}/2)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\iota_{N}\mathcal{E}^{s}+\frac{8}{\varsigma}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{P}[H_{1}(X)^{2}]\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma r_{0}(X))]\right\}^{1/2}\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}/4)

\displaystyle+\frac{4d_{y}e}{\varsigma}(\delta^{*}+1)\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma r_{0}(X))]\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}/2).

Here,
H_{1}(X^{s})
attains a finite second moment since
\|Y^{s}\|_{2}
is presumed to have a finite fourth moment. Set
\iota_{N}
to
(4\varsigma^{-1}\log N)\vee 1
. We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{t}\leq

\displaystyle\iota_{N}\mathcal{E}^{s}+\frac{8}{\varsigma}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{P}[H_{1}(X)^{2}]\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma r_{0}(X))]\right\}^{1/2}\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}/4)

\displaystyle+\frac{4d_{y}e}{\varsigma}(\delta^{*}+1)\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma r_{0}(X))]\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}/2)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left[(4\varsigma^{-1}\log N)\vee 1\right]\mathcal{E}^{s}+\frac{8}{\varsigma}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{P}[H_{1}(X)^{2}]\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma r_{0}(X))]\right\}^{1/2}N^{-1}

\displaystyle+\frac{4d_{y}e}{\varsigma}(\delta^{*}+1)\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma r_{0}(X))]N^{-2}.

This completes the proof.
∎

B.9
Proof of Theorem 5.4

For any function
f\in\mathcal{L}^{2}(X^{s},Y^{s})
, define

\displaystyle K^{\mathrm{gen}}(f)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\|\dot{a}_{\tau}\eta+\dot{b}_{\tau}Y-f(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)\right\|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau,

\displaystyle K^{\mathrm{gen}}_{N}(f)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left\|\dot{a}_{\tau_{i}}\eta_{i}+\dot{b}_{\tau_{i}}Y^{s}_{i}-f(X^{s}_{i},Y^{s}_{i,\tau_{i}},\tau_{i})\right\|_{2}^{2}.

Here,
\mathbb{E}_{P}[h(X,Y,\eta,Y_{\tau})]\equiv\mathbb{E}[h(X^{s},Y^{s},\eta,Y^{s}_{\tau})]
for any
(X^{s},Y^{s},\eta)
-integrable function
h
, where the expectation is taken with respect to
(X^{s},Y^{s},\eta)
for any nonrandom
\tau\in[0,1]
.

Lemma B.10
.

Assume that
\|Y^{s}\|_{\infty}
attains a finite second moment. Then,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\|\hat{v}^{s}_{N}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[K^{\mathrm{gen}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N})-2K^{\mathrm{gen}}_{N}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N})+K^{\mathrm{gen}}(v_{0})\right]

\displaystyle+2\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN},\Lambda}^{d_{y}}}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}_{P}\|f(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau.

Recall that
Y^{s}_{\tau}=a_{\tau}\eta+b_{\tau}Y^{s}
and
v_{0}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)=\mathbb{E}(\dot{a}_{\tau}\eta+\dot{b}_{\tau}Y^{s}|Y^{s}_{\tau},X^{s})
. For any
\tau\in[0,1]
, we have

\mathbb{E}\|v_{0}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\|\dot{a}_{\tau}\eta+\dot{b}_{\tau}Y\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\dot{a}_{\tau}^{2}\mathbb{E}\|\eta\|_{2}^{2}+2\dot{b}_{\tau}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{P}\|Y\|_{2}^{2}.

Given that
\|Y^{s}\|_{\infty}
has a finite second moment, we obtain
\mathbb{E}_{P}\|Y\|_{2}^{2}<\infty
and hence
v_{0}\in\mathcal{L}^{2}(X^{s},Y^{s})
. Furthermore, as
a_{\tau}
and
b_{\tau}
are presumed to be continuously differentiable, notice that

\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\|v_{0}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau\leq\int_{0}^{1}\left(2\dot{a}_{\tau}^{2}\mathbb{E}\|\eta\|_{2}^{2}+2\dot{b}_{\tau}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{P}\|Y\|_{2}^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}\tau<\infty.

Next, for any
f\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN},\Lambda}^{d_{y}}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\|\hat{v}^{s}_{N}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[K^{\mathrm{gen}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N})-K^{\mathrm{gen}}(v_{0})]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[K^{\mathrm{gen}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N})-K^{\mathrm{gen}}(v_{0})]+2\mathbb{E}[K^{\mathrm{gen}}_{N}(f)-K^{\mathrm{gen}}_{N}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[K^{\mathrm{gen}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N})-K^{\mathrm{gen}}(v_{0})]+2\mathbb{E}[K^{\mathrm{gen}}_{N}(f)-K^{\mathrm{gen}}_{N}(v_{0})+K^{\mathrm{gen}}_{N}(v_{0})-K^{\mathrm{gen}}_{N}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[K^{\mathrm{gen}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N})-2K^{\mathrm{gen}}_{N}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N})+K^{\mathrm{gen}}(v_{0})]+2[K^{\mathrm{gen}}(f)-K^{\mathrm{gen}}(v_{0})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[K^{\mathrm{gen}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N})-2K^{\mathrm{gen}}_{N}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N})+K^{\mathrm{gen}}(v_{0})]+2\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}_{P}\|f(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau.

Taking infimum with respect to
f\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN},\Lambda}^{d_{y}}
on the both sides, we obtain the result.
∎

Lemma B.11
.

Assume that
\|Y^{s}\|_{\infty}
is a sub-Gaussian random variable. Let
\bar{\delta}=\bar{\delta}_{N}=(\log N)^{(1+\kappa)/2}
, with an arbitrarily fixed
\kappa\in(0,1)
, and let
\underline{\delta}=\underline{\delta}_{N}=-(\log N)^{(1+\kappa)/2}
. Then, for sufficiently large
N
and
N\geq\mathrm{Pdim}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN}})
, it follows that

\mathbb{E}[K^{\mathrm{gen}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N})-2K^{\mathrm{gen}}_{N}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N})+K^{\mathrm{gen}}(v_{0})]\leq\frac{c^{*}SL\log S(\log N)^{3+2\kappa}}{N},

where
c^{*}
is a constant not depending on
S,L
and
N
.

Let
T_{i}=(X^{s}_{i},Y^{s}_{i},\eta_{i},\tau_{i})
for
i=1,\dots,N
,
\mathcal{D}_{N}=\{T_{1},\dots,T_{N}\}
, and
T=(X^{s},Y^{s},\eta,\tau)
be an independent copy of
T_{1}
. Firstly, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[K^{\mathrm{gen}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N})-2K^{\mathrm{gen}}_{N}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N})+K^{\mathrm{gen}}(v_{0})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{N}}[K^{\mathrm{gen}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N})-2K^{\mathrm{gen}}_{N}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N})+K^{\mathrm{gen}}(v_{0})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{N}}\{K^{\mathrm{gen}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N})-K^{\mathrm{gen}}(v_{0})-2[K^{\mathrm{gen}}_{N}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N})-K^{\mathrm{gen}}_{N}(v_{0})]\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{T}[g(\hat{v}^{s}_{N},T)]-\frac{2}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g(\hat{v}^{s}_{N},T_{i})\right\},

where
g(f,T)=\|\dot{a}_{\tau}\eta+\dot{b}_{\tau}Y^{s}-f(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}-\|\dot{a}_{\tau}\eta+\dot{b}_{\tau}Y^{s}-v_{0}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}
for
f\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN},\Lambda}^{d_{y}}
. For a
d_{y}
-dimensional vector
v
, denote its
j
-th component as
v_{(j)}
; additionally, we denote the
j
-th output coordinate of a function
f:\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{y}}\times[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{y}}
as
f_{(j)}
, with
j\in\{1,\dots,d_{y}\}
. Furthermore, for any measurable function
h:\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{y}}\times[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}
, define

g_{j}(h,T)=[\dot{a}_{\tau}\eta_{(j)}+\dot{b}_{\tau}Y^{s}_{(j)}-h(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)]^{2}-[\dot{a}_{\tau}\eta_{(j)}+\dot{b}_{\tau}Y^{s}_{(j)}-v_{0}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)_{(j)}]^{2},

for
j=1,\dots,d_{y}
. It is then clear that
g(f,T)=\sum_{j=1}^{d_{y}}g_{j}(f_{(j)},T)
. Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[K^{\mathrm{gen}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N})-2K^{\mathrm{gen}}_{N}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N})+K^{\mathrm{gen}}(v_{0})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{T}[g(\hat{v}^{s}_{N},T)]-\frac{2}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g(\hat{v}^{s}_{N},T_{i})\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{d_{y}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{T}[g_{j}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N,(j)},T)]-\frac{2}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g_{j}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N,(j)},T_{i})\right\}.

Subsequently, let us fix an arbitrary
j\in\{1,\dots,d_{y}\}
. For any
\iota_{N}\geq 1
, we let

\displaystyle U_{0}

\displaystyle=\dot{a}_{\tau}\eta_{(j)}+\dot{b}_{\tau}Y^{s}_{(j)},

\displaystyle U_{1}

\displaystyle=(\dot{a}_{\tau}\eta_{(j)}+\dot{b}_{\tau}Y^{s}_{(j)})\mathds{1}(\|\eta\|_{\infty}+\|Y^{s}\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N}),

\displaystyle U_{2}

\displaystyle=\|\eta\|_{\infty}+\|Y^{s}\|_{\infty},

\displaystyle V_{0}

\displaystyle=v_{0}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)_{(j)},

\displaystyle V_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[(\dot{a}_{\tau}\eta_{(j)}+\dot{b}_{\tau}Y^{s}_{(j)})\mathds{1}(\|\eta\|_{\infty}+\|Y^{s}\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N})\Big|Y^{s}_{\tau},X^{s},\tau\right].

Then, for any measurable function
h:\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{y}}\times[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}
, define

\displaystyle g_{j,\iota_{N}}(h,T)

\displaystyle=[U_{1}-h(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)]^{2}-(U_{1}-V_{1})^{2}

\displaystyle=[V_{1}-h(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)][2U_{1}-h(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)-V_{1}].

Since
\eta
is a standard Gaussian random vector,
\|\eta\|_{\infty}^{2}
follows a sub-exponential distribution. To see this, note that
\|\eta\|_{\infty}^{2}=\max(\eta_{(1)}^{2},\dots,\eta_{(d_{y})}^{2})
and
\eta_{(j)}^{2}
follows a Gamma distribution with a shape parameter 1/2 and a rate parameter 1/2 for all
j\in\{1,\dots,d_{y}\}
. Hence, for any
\omega\in(0,1/2)
, we have
\mathbb{E}[\exp(\omega\eta_{(j)}^{2})]=(1-2\omega)^{-1/2}<\infty
for all
j\in\{1,\dots,d_{y}\}
. This indicates that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\exp(\omega d_{y}^{-1}\|\eta\|_{\infty}^{2})]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\exp\left(\frac{1}{d_{y}}\sum_{j=1}^{d_{y}}\omega\eta_{(j)}^{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{d_{y}}\sum_{j=1}^{d_{y}}\mathbb{E}[\exp(\omega\eta_{(j)}^{2})]=(1-2\omega)^{-1/2}<\infty.

On the other hand, we have assumed that
\|Y^{s}\|_{\infty}
is a sub-Gaussian random variable. Consequently, there exists a constant
\varsigma
(which possibly associates with
d_{y}
) such that
\mathbb{E}[\exp(\varsigma\|\eta\|_{\infty}^{2})]<\infty
and
\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|Y\|_{\infty}^{2})]<\infty
. Hence, observe that

\mathbb{E}[\exp(\varsigma U_{2}^{2}/2)]\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|\eta\|_{\infty}^{2}+\varsigma\|Y\|_{\infty}^{2})]=\mathbb{E}[\exp(\varsigma\|\eta\|_{\infty}^{2})]\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|Y\|_{\infty}^{2})]<\infty.

Additionally, as
a_{\tau}
and
b_{\tau}
are presumed to be continuously differentiable over
[0,1]
, we let
\gamma=\max_{\xi\in[0,1]}\max(|\dot{a}_{\xi}|,|\dot{b}_{\xi}|)
. Then, it follows that

\displaystyle|g_{j}(f_{(j)},T)-g_{j,\iota_{N}}(f_{(j)},T)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|[U_{0}-f(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)_{(j)}]^{2}-[U_{1}-f(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)_{(j)}]^{2}\right|+\left|(U_{0}-V_{0})^{2}-(U_{1}-V_{1})^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|(U_{1}-U_{0})[U_{0}+U_{1}-2f(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)_{(j)}]\right|

\displaystyle+\left|(U_{0}-U_{1}+V_{1}-V_{0})(U_{0}+U_{1}-V_{0}-V_{1})\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\gamma U_{2}\mathds{1}(U_{2}>\iota_{N})(\gamma U_{2}+\gamma\iota_{N}+2\bar{\delta}_{N})

\displaystyle+\gamma U_{2}\mathds{1}(U_{2}>\iota_{N})[\gamma U_{2}+2\gamma\iota_{N}+\|v_{0}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)\|_{\infty}]

\displaystyle+\gamma\mathbb{E}[U_{2}\mathds{1}(U_{2}>\iota_{N})|Y^{s}_{\tau},X^{s},\tau][\gamma U_{2}+2\gamma\iota_{N}+\|v_{0}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)\|_{\infty}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma U_{2}[2\gamma U_{2}+3\gamma\iota_{N}+2\bar{\delta}_{N}+\|v_{0}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)\|_{\infty}]\mathds{1}(U_{2}>\iota_{N})

\displaystyle+\gamma\mathbb{E}[U_{2}\mathds{1}(U_{2}>\iota_{N})|Y^{s}_{\tau},X^{s},\tau][\gamma U_{2}+2\gamma\iota_{N}+\|v_{0}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)\|_{\infty}].

Taking expectation, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{T}[g_{j}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N,(j)},T)]-\frac{2}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g_{j}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N,(j)},T_{i})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{T}[g_{j,\iota_{N}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N,(j)},T)]-\frac{2}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g_{j,\iota_{N}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N,(j)},T_{i})\right\}

\displaystyle+3\gamma\mathbb{E}\left\{U_{2}[2\gamma U_{2}+3\gamma\iota_{N}+2\bar{\delta}_{N}+\|v_{0}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)\|_{\infty}]\mathds{1}(U_{2}>\iota_{N})\right\}

\displaystyle+3\gamma\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathbb{E}[U_{2}\mathds{1}(U_{2}>\iota_{N})|Y^{s}_{\tau},X^{s},\tau][\gamma U_{2}+2\gamma\iota_{N}+\|v_{0}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)\|_{\infty}]\right\}.

Specifically, observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{U_{2}[2\gamma U_{2}+3\gamma\iota_{N}+2\bar{\delta}_{N}+\|v_{0}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)\|_{\infty}]\mathds{1}(U_{2}>\iota_{N})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{8\gamma}{\varsigma}\mathbb{E}[\exp(\varsigma U_{2}^{2}/2)]\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}^{2}/4)+\frac{4}{\varsigma}(3\gamma\iota_{N}+2\bar{\delta}_{N})\mathbb{E}[\exp(\varsigma U_{2}^{2}/2)]\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}^{2}/4)

\displaystyle+\frac{8}{\varsigma}\mathbb{E}[\|v_{0}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)\|_{\infty}\exp(\varsigma U_{2}^{2}/4)]\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}^{2}/8)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{8\gamma}{\varsigma}\mathbb{E}[\exp(\varsigma U_{2}^{2}/2)]\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}^{2}/4)+\frac{4}{\varsigma}(3\gamma\iota_{N}+2\bar{\delta}_{N})\mathbb{E}[\exp(\varsigma U_{2}^{2}/2)]\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}^{2}/4)

\displaystyle+\frac{8}{\varsigma}\left\{\mathbb{E}[\|v_{0}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)\|_{\infty}^{2}]\mathbb{E}[\exp(\varsigma U_{2}^{2}/2)]\right\}^{1/2}\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}^{2}/8)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{8\gamma}{\varsigma}\mathbb{E}[\exp(\varsigma U_{2}^{2}/2)]\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}^{2}/4)+\frac{4}{\varsigma}(3\gamma\iota_{N}+2\bar{\delta}_{N})\mathbb{E}[\exp(\varsigma U_{2}^{2}/2)]\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}^{2}/4)

\displaystyle+\frac{8\gamma}{\varsigma}\left\{\mathbb{E}(U_{2}^{2})\mathbb{E}[\exp(\varsigma U_{2}^{2}/2)]\right\}^{1/2}\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}^{2}/8),

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\{\mathbb{E}[U_{2}\mathds{1}(U_{2}>\iota_{N})|Y^{s}_{\tau},X^{s},\tau][\gamma U_{2}+2\gamma\iota_{N}+\|v_{0}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)\|_{\infty}]\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\gamma\left\{\mathbb{E}(U_{2}^{2})\mathbb{E}[U_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}(U_{2}>\iota_{N})]\right\}^{1/2}+2\gamma\iota_{N}\mathbb{E}[U_{2}\mathds{1}(U_{2}>\iota_{N})]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}[\|v_{0}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)\|_{\infty}U_{2}\mathds{1}(U_{2}>\iota_{N})]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2\gamma}{\varsigma^{1/2}}\left\{\mathbb{E}(U_{2}^{2})\mathbb{E}[\exp(\varsigma U_{2}^{2}/2)\right\}^{1/2}\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}^{2}/8)+\frac{8\gamma}{\varsigma}\iota_{N}\mathbb{E}[\exp(\varsigma U_{2}^{2}/2)\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}^{2}/4)

\displaystyle+\frac{8}{\varsigma}\mathbb{E}[\|v_{0}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)\|_{\infty}\exp(\varsigma U_{2}^{2}/4)]\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}^{2}/8)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2\gamma}{\varsigma^{1/2}}\left\{\mathbb{E}(U_{2}^{2})\mathbb{E}[\exp(\varsigma U_{2}^{2}/2)\right\}^{1/2}\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}^{2}/8)+\frac{8\gamma}{\varsigma}\iota_{N}\mathbb{E}[\exp(\varsigma U_{2}^{2}/2)\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}^{2}/4)

\displaystyle+\frac{8\gamma}{\varsigma}\left\{\mathbb{E}(U_{2}^{2})\mathbb{E}[\exp(\varsigma U_{2}^{2}/2)]\right\}^{1/2}\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}^{2}/8).

Here, we have applied the inequalities
a\leq\exp(a)
and
\mathds{1}(a>0)\leq\exp(a)
for
a\in\mathbb{R}
. As a consequence, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{T}[g_{j}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N,(j)},T)]-\frac{2}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g_{j}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N,(j)},T_{i})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{T}[g_{j,\iota_{N}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N,(j)},T)]-\frac{2}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g_{j,\iota_{N}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N,(j)},T_{i})\right\}+c_{1}(\iota_{N}+\bar{\delta}_{N}+1)\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}^{2}/8),

where
c_{1}
is a constant which depends only on
\gamma,\varsigma
and
\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|Y\|_{\infty}^{2})]
. Recall that
\bar{\delta}_{N}=(\log N)^{(1+\kappa)/2}
. Set
\iota_{N}
to
(8\varsigma^{-1}\log N)^{1/2}\vee 1
. Then, for sufficiently large
N
such that
\bar{\delta}_{N}\geq\iota_{N}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{T}[g_{j}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N,(j)},T)]-\frac{2}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g_{j}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N,(j)},T_{i})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{T}[g_{j,\iota_{N}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N,(j)},T)]-\frac{2}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g_{j,\iota_{N}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N,(j)},T_{i})\right\}+3c_{1}(\log N)^{(1+\kappa)/2}N^{-1}.

Furthermore, we proceed to verify the conditions in Theorem
A.6
. Notice that

\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN},\Lambda}^{d_{y}},T\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+2d_{y}}\times[0,1]}|g_{j,\iota_{N}}(f_{(j)},T)|\leq 6\gamma^{2}\iota_{N}^{2}+2\bar{\delta}_{N}^{2}\leq 8\bar{\delta}_{N}^{2}=8(\log N)^{1+\kappa},

whenever
\bar{\delta}_{N}\geq(\gamma\vee 1)\iota_{N}
. Furthermore, for any
f\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN},\Lambda}^{d_{y}}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[g_{j,\iota_{N}}(f_{(j)},T)]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\{[V_{1}-f(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)_{(j)}][2U_{1}-f(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)_{(j)}-V_{1}]\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[V_{1}-f(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)_{(j)}]^{2},

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[g_{j,\iota_{N}}(f,T)^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left\{[V_{1}-f(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)_{(j)}]^{2}[2U_{1}-f(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)_{(j)}-V_{1}]^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq(3\gamma\iota_{N}+\bar{\delta}_{N})^{2}\mathbb{E}[V_{1}-f(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)_{(j)}]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 16\bar{\delta}_{N}^{2}\mathbb{E}[g_{j,\iota_{N}}(f_{(j)},T)]

\displaystyle=16(\log N)^{1+\kappa}\mathbb{E}[g_{j,\iota_{N}}(f_{(j)},T)],

provided that
N
is sufficiently large such that
\bar{\delta}_{N}\geq(\gamma\vee 1)\iota_{N}
. Hence, Theorem
A.6
suggests that, for sufficiently large
N
such that
\bar{\delta}_{N}\geq(\gamma\vee 1)\iota_{N}
, with
N\geq\mathrm{Pdim}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN}})
, and for arbitrary
t>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{T}[g_{j,\iota_{N}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N,(j)},T)]-\frac{2}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g_{j,\iota_{N}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N,(j)},T_{i})\geq t\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{T}[g_{j,\iota_{N}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N,(j)},T)]-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g_{j,\iota_{N}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N,(j)},T_{i})\geq\frac{1}{2}\left\{\frac{t}{2}+\frac{t}{2}+\mathbb{E}_{T}[g_{j,\iota_{N}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N,(j)},T)]\right\}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left(\exists h\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN}}:\mathbb{E}[g_{j,\iota_{N}}(h,T)]-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g_{j,\iota_{N}}(h,T_{i})\geq\frac{1}{2}\left\{\frac{t}{2}+\frac{t}{2}+\mathbb{E}_{T}[g_{j,\iota_{N}}(h,T)]\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 14\mathcal{N}_{N}\left(c_{2}t,\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\{g_{j,\iota_{N}}(h,\cdot):\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{y}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{y}}\times[0,1]\to\mathbb{R},h\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN}}\}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{Nt}{c_{3}(\log N)^{2+2\kappa}}\right),

where
c_{2},c_{3}
are universal constants. Subsequently, we bound the covering number. Fix
\{x_{1},\dots,x_{N}\}\subset(\mathcal{R}^{d_{x}})^{N}
,
\{y_{1},\dots,y_{N}\}\subset(\mathcal{R}^{d_{y}})^{N}
,
\{z_{1},\dots,z_{N}\}\subset(\mathcal{R}^{d_{y}})^{N}
and
\{\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{N}\}\in[0,1]^{N}
. Let
\mathcal{C}=\{(x_{1},\zeta_{1},\xi_{1}),\dots,(x_{N},\zeta_{N},\xi_{N})\}
where
\zeta_{i}=a_{\xi_{i}}z_{i}+b_{\xi_{i}}y_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,N
. Let
w^{\sharp}=\{w_{1},\dots,w_{k}\}
be an
\epsilon
-covering set of
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN}|\mathcal{C}}
where
w_{i}=h_{i|\mathcal{C}}
for some
h_{i}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN}}\ (i=1,\dots,k)
, such that for any
h\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN}}
, there exists
w^{*}=h^{*}_{|\mathcal{C}}\in w^{\sharp}
satisfying
\|w^{*}-h_{|\mathcal{C}}\|_{\infty}<\epsilon
. This indicates

\displaystyle|g_{j,\iota_{N}}(h,(x_{i},y_{i},z_{i},\xi_{i}))-g_{j,\iota_{N}}(h^{*},(x_{i},y_{i},z_{i},\xi_{i}))|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|h^{*}(x_{i},\zeta_{i},\xi_{i})-h(x_{i},\zeta_{i},\xi_{i})|\cdot(2\gamma\iota_{N}+2\bar{\delta}_{N})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2(\gamma\iota_{N}+\bar{\delta}_{N})\epsilon

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\bar{\delta}_{N}\epsilon,

whenever
\bar{\delta}_{N}\geq(\gamma\vee 1)\iota_{N}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{N}(c_{2}t,\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\{g_{j,\iota_{N}}(h,\cdot):\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{y}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{y}}\times[0,1]\to\mathbb{R},h\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN}}\})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{N}(c_{2}t/(4\bar{\delta}_{N}),\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN}}).

Then, with Lemma
A.4
and Lemma
A.5
, for sufficiently large
N
with
N\geq\mathrm{Pdim}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN}})
and any
a_{N}\geq 1/N
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{T}[g_{j,\iota_{N}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N,(j)},T)]-\frac{2}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g_{j,\iota_{N}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N,(j)},T_{i})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle a_{N}+14\int_{a_{N}}^{\infty}\mathcal{N}_{N}(c_{2}t/(4\bar{\delta}_{N}),\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN}})\exp\left(-\frac{Nt}{c_{3}(\log N)^{2+2\kappa}}\right)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle a_{N}+14\mathcal{N}_{N}(c_{2}a_{N}/(4\bar{\delta}_{N}),\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN}})\int_{a_{N}}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{Nt}{c_{3}(\log N)^{2+2\kappa}}\right)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle a_{N}+14\left(c_{4}N^{2}\bar{\delta}_{N}^{2}\right)^{c_{5}SL\log S}\cdot\frac{c_{3}(\log N)^{2+2\kappa}}{N}\exp\left(-\frac{Na_{N}}{c_{3}(\log N)^{2+2\kappa}}\right),

where
c_{4}
and
c_{5}
are universal constants. Choose

a_{N}=\frac{c_{3}c_{5}(\log N)^{2+2\kappa}}{N}SL\log S\log\left(c_{4}N^{2}\bar{\delta}_{N}^{2}\right).

For sufficiently large
N
, we have

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{D}_{N}}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{T}[g_{j,\iota_{N}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N,(j)},T)]-\frac{2}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}g_{j,\iota_{N}}(\hat{v}^{s}_{N,(j)},T_{i})\right\}\leq\frac{c_{6}SL\log S(\log N)^{3+2\kappa}}{N},

where
c_{6}
is a constant not depending on
S,L
and
N
. Noticing the arbitrariness of
j
, we complete the proof.
∎

Lemma B.12
.

Assume that

(i)

e_{j}^{\top}v_{0}\in\mathcal{W}^{1,\infty}_{\mathrm{Gen}}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{y}},B_{u})
with
B_{u}\leq c(u^{m}+1)
for some universal constants
c>0
,
m\in[0,1]
, and for any
j\in\{1,\dots,d_{y}\}
, where
e_{j}
denotes a
d_{y}
-dimensional one-hot vector with the
j
-th component equal to 1 and all other components equal to 0;

(ii)

\|Y^{s}\|_{\infty}
and
\|X^{s}\|_{\infty}
follow sub-Gaussian distributions.

Suppose that the depth
L
and width
M
of
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN},\Lambda}^{d_{y}}
satisfy

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}(d_{x}+d_{y}+1)^{2}S_{1}\log S_{1}+3,

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}2^{d_{x}+d_{y}+1}d_{y}(d_{x}+d_{y}+1)S_{2}\log S_{2},

for any
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, where
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
are universal constants. Let
\bar{\delta}=\bar{\delta}_{N}=(\log N)^{(1+\kappa)/2}
, with an arbitrarily fixed
\kappa\in(0,1)
, and let
\underline{\delta}=\underline{\delta}_{N}=-(\log N)^{-(1+\kappa)/2}
,
\Lambda=\Lambda_{N}=(\log N)^{(1+\kappa)/2}
. Then, for sufficiently large
N
, it follows that

\displaystyle\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN},\Lambda}^{d_{y}}}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}_{P}\|f(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c^{*}\left\{\left[(S_{1}S_{2})^{-2/(d_{x}+d_{y}+1)}(\log N)^{m/2}\right]^{2}+\frac{(\log N)^{1+\kappa}}{N}\right\},

where
c^{*}
is a constant not depending on
S_{1},S_{2}
and
N
.

Let
\gamma_{1}=\max_{\tau\in[0,1]}\max(|a_{\tau}|,|b_{\tau}|)\vee 1
and

\gamma_{2}=\max_{\tau\in[0,1]}\max(|\dot{a}_{\tau}|,|\dot{b}_{\tau}|),

both of which are well-defined as we presume that
a_{\tau}
and
b_{\tau}
are continuously differentiable over
[0,1]
. For any
\iota_{N}\geq 1
and
\tau\in[0,1]
, observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\|f(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{\|f(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}<\iota_{N})\mathds{1}((\|\eta\|_{\infty}+\|Y\|_{\infty})<\gamma_{1}^{-1}\iota_{N})\right\}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{\|f(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq\iota_{N})\right\}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{\|f(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}<\iota_{N})\mathds{1}((\|\eta\|_{\infty}+\|Y\|_{\infty})\geq\gamma_{1}^{-1}\iota_{N})\right\}.

As we assume that
\|Y^{s}\|_{\infty}
and
\|X^{s}\|_{\infty}
follow sub-Gaussian distributions,
\|Y^{s}\|_{\infty}^{2}
and
\|X^{s}\|_{\infty}^{2}
are sub-exponentially distributed random variables. This indicates the existence of a constant
\varsigma
such that
\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|Y\|_{\infty}^{2})]<\infty
,
\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|X\|_{\infty}^{2})]<\infty
and
\mathbb{E}[\exp(\varsigma\|\eta\|_{\infty}^{2})]<\infty
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma(\|\eta\|_{\infty}+\|Y\|_{\infty})^{2}/2)]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|\eta\|_{\infty}^{2}+\varsigma\|Y\|_{\infty}^{2})]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[\exp(\varsigma\|\eta\|_{\infty}^{2})]\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|Y\|_{\infty}^{2})].

For clarity, denote the
j
-th output coordinate of a function
f:\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}}\times\mathbb{R}^{d_{y}}\times[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}^{d_{y}}
as
f_{(j)}
, with
j\in\{1,\dots,d_{y}\}
. It then follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{\|f(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq\iota_{N})\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{d_{y}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{[f_{(j)}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0,(j)}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)]^{2}\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq\iota_{N})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2d_{y}\bar{\delta}_{N}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{P}[\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq\iota_{N})]+2d_{y}\mathbb{E}_{P}[\|v_{0}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)\|_{\infty}^{2}\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq\iota_{N})]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2d_{y}\bar{\delta}_{N}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|X\|_{\infty}^{2}/2)]\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}^{2}/2)

\displaystyle+2d_{y}\mathbb{E}_{P}[\|v_{0}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)\|_{\infty}^{2}\exp(\varsigma\|X\|_{\infty}^{2}/2)]\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}^{2}/2)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2d_{y}\bar{\delta}_{N}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|X\|_{\infty}^{2}/2)]\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}^{2}/2)

\displaystyle+2d_{y}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{P}[\|v_{0}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)\|_{\infty}^{4}]\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|X\|_{\infty}^{2})]\right\}^{1/2}\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}^{2}/2)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2d_{y}\bar{\delta}_{N}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|X\|_{\infty}^{2}/2)]\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}^{2}/2)

\displaystyle+8d_{y}\gamma_{2}^{2}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{P}(\|\eta\|_{\infty}^{4}+\|Y\|_{\infty}^{4})\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|X\|_{\infty}^{2})]\right\}^{1/2}\exp(-\varsigma\iota_{N}^{2}/2),

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{\|f(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}<\iota_{N})\mathds{1}((\|\eta\|_{\infty}+\|Y\|_{\infty})\geq\gamma_{1}^{-1}\iota_{N})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{\|f(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}((\|\eta\|_{\infty}+\|Y\|_{\infty})\geq\gamma_{1}^{-1}\iota_{N})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2d_{y}\bar{\delta}_{N}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{P}[\mathds{1}((\|\eta\|_{\infty}+\|Y\|_{\infty})\geq\gamma_{1}^{-1}\iota_{N})]

\displaystyle+2d_{y}\mathbb{E}_{P}[\|v_{0}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)\|_{\infty}^{2}\mathds{1}((\|\eta\|_{\infty}+\|Y\|_{\infty})\geq\gamma_{1}^{-1}\iota_{N})]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2d_{y}\bar{\delta}_{N}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma(\|\eta\|_{\infty}+\|Y\|_{\infty})^{2}/4)]\exp(-\varsigma\gamma_{1}^{-2}\iota_{N}^{2}/4)

\displaystyle+8d_{y}\gamma_{2}^{2}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{P}(\|\eta\|_{\infty}^{4}+\|Y\|_{\infty}^{4})\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma(\|\eta\|_{\infty}+\|Y\|_{\infty})^{2}/2)]\right\}^{1/2}\exp(-\varsigma\gamma_{1}^{-2}\iota_{N}^{2}/4)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2d_{y}\bar{\delta}_{N}^{2}\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|\eta\|_{\infty}^{2}/2)]\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|Y\|_{\infty}^{2}/2)]\exp(-\varsigma\gamma_{1}^{-2}\iota_{N}^{2}/4)

\displaystyle+8d_{y}\gamma_{2}^{2}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{P}(\|\eta\|_{\infty}^{4}+\|Y\|_{\infty}^{4})\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|\eta\|_{\infty}^{2})]\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|Y\|_{\infty}^{2})]\right\}^{1/2}\exp(-\varsigma\gamma_{1}^{-2}\iota_{N}^{2}/4).

Hence, we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{\|f(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}\geq\iota_{N})\right\}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{\|f(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}<\iota_{N})\mathds{1}((\|\eta\|_{\infty}+\|Y\|_{\infty})\geq\gamma_{1}^{-1}\iota_{N})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{1}(\bar{\delta}_{N}^{2}+1)\exp(-\varsigma\gamma_{1}^{-2}\iota_{N}^{2}/4),

where
c_{1}
is a constant which depends only on
d_{y},\gamma_{2},\varsigma,\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|X\|_{\infty}^{2})]
and
\mathbb{E}_{P}[\exp(\varsigma\|Y\|_{\infty}^{2}/2)]
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\|f(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\left\{\|f(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}<\iota_{N})\mathds{1}((\|\eta\|_{\infty}+\|Y\|_{\infty})<\gamma_{1}^{-1}\iota_{N})\right\}

\displaystyle+c_{1}(\bar{\delta}_{N}^{2}+1)\exp(-\varsigma\gamma_{1}^{-2}\iota_{N}^{2}/4).

Next, let us focus on the region

\{(x,y,\tau):\|x\|_{\infty}<\iota_{N},\|y\|_{\infty}<\iota_{N},\tau\in(0,1)\}=(-\iota_{N},\iota_{N})^{d_{x}+d_{y}}\times(0,1).

Fix an arbitrary
j\in\{1,\dots,d_{y}\}
. Let

h_{j}(x,y,\tau)=v_{0,(j)}\left(2\iota_{N}x-\iota_{N}\mathrm{1}_{d_{x}},2\iota_{N}y-\iota_{N}\mathrm{1}_{d_{y}},\tau\right),

for
(x,y,\tau)\in(0,1)^{d_{x}}\times(0,1)^{d_{y}}\times(0,1)
. By the assumption that each component of
v_{0}
belongs to
\mathcal{W}^{1,\infty}_{\mathrm{Gen}}(\mathbb{R}^{d_{x}+d_{y}},B_{u})
with
B_{u}\leq c(u^{m}+1)
, we have
\|h_{j}\|_{\mathcal{W}^{1,\infty}}((0,1)^{d_{x}+d_{y}+1})\leq c(\iota_{N}^{m}+1)
, where the constants
c>0
,
m\in[0,1]
. Lemma
A.3
demonstrates that for any
S_{1},S_{2}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, there exists a function
h_{j}^{*}
implemented by a ReLU network with depth
L^{*}\leq(d_{x}+d_{y}+1)^{2}S_{1}\log S_{1}
, width
M^{*}\leq 2^{d_{x}+d_{y}+1}(d_{x}+d_{y}+1)S_{2}\log S_{2}
, such that
\|h^{*}\|_{\mathcal{W}^{1,\infty}((0,1)^{d_{x}+d_{y}+1})}\leq c_{2}(\iota_{N}^{m}+1)
and

|h_{j}^{*}(x,y,\tau)-h_{j}(x,y,\tau)|\leq c_{3}(\iota_{N}^{m}+1)(S_{1}S_{2})^{-2/(d_{x}+d_{y}+1)},

for all
(x,y,\tau)\in(0,1)^{d_{x}}\times(0,1)^{d_{y}}\times(0,1)
, where
c_{2}
and
c_{3}
are constants which depend only on
d_{x}
and
d_{y}
. Let

h_{j}^{\dagger}(x,y,\tau)=h_{j}^{*}\left((x+\iota_{N}\mathrm{1}_{d_{x}})/(2\iota_{N}),(y+\iota_{N}\mathrm{1}_{d_{y}})/(2\iota_{N}),\tau\right),

for
(x,y,\tau)\in(-\iota_{N},\iota_{N})^{d_{x}}\times(-\iota_{N},\iota_{N})^{d_{y}}\times(0,1)
. We obtain that

|h_{j}^{\dagger}(x,y,\tau)-v_{0,(j)}(x,y,\tau)|\leq c_{3}(\iota_{N}^{m}+1)(S_{1}S_{2})^{-2/(d_{x}+d_{y}+1)},

for all
(x,y,\tau)\in(-\iota_{N},\iota_{N})^{d_{x}}\times(-\iota_{N},\iota_{N})^{d_{y}}\times(0,1)
. Furthermore, note that

\displaystyle h_{j}^{\dagger}(x,y,\tau)=

\displaystyle h_{j}^{*}\left(\frac{x+\iota_{N}\mathrm{1}_{d_{x}}}{2\iota_{N}},\frac{y+\iota_{N}\mathrm{1}_{d_{y}}}{2\iota_{N}},\tau\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle h_{j}^{*}\bigg(\mathrm{relu}\left(\frac{x+\iota_{N}\mathrm{1}_{d_{x}}}{2\iota_{N}}\right)-\mathrm{relu}\left(-\frac{x+\iota_{N}\mathrm{1}_{d_{x}}}{2\iota_{N}}\right),

\displaystyle\mathrm{relu}\left(\frac{y+\iota_{N}\mathrm{1}_{d_{y}}}{2\iota_{N}}\right)-\mathrm{relu}\left(-\frac{y+\iota_{N}\mathrm{1}_{d_{y}}}{2\iota_{N}}\right),

\displaystyle\mathrm{relu}(\tau)-\mathrm{relu}(-\tau)\bigg),

which is implemented by a neural network with ReLU activations, depth
L^{\dagger}=L^{*}+1
, width
M^{\dagger}=M^{*}
and Lipschitz constant no more than
c_{2}(d_{x}+d_{y}+1)(\iota_{N}^{m}+1)
. In addition, let

h_{j}^{\ddagger}(x,y,\tau)=\begin{cases}\bar{\delta}_{N},&h_{j}^{\dagger}(x,y,\tau)>\bar{\delta}_{N},\\
h_{j}^{\dagger}(x,y,\tau),&\underline{\delta}_{N}\leq h_{j}^{\dagger}(x,y,\tau)\leq\bar{\delta}_{N},\\
\underline{\delta}_{N},&h_{j}^{\dagger}(x,y,\tau)<\underline{\delta}_{N}.\end{cases}

A straightforward calculation shows that

h_{j}^{\ddagger}(x,y,\tau)=\mathrm{relu}(-\mathrm{relu}(-h_{j}^{\dagger}(x,y,\tau)+\bar{\delta}_{N})+\bar{\delta}_{N})-\mathrm{relu}(-\mathrm{relu}(h_{j}^{\dagger}(x,y,\tau)-\underline{\delta}_{N})-\underline{\delta}_{N}),

indicating that
h_{j}^{\ddagger}(x,y,\tau)
can be implemented by a ReLU network with depth
L^{\ddagger}=L^{*}+3
, width
M^{\ddagger}=M^{*}
and Lipschitz constant no more than
2c_{2}(d_{x}+d_{y}+1)(\iota_{N}^{m}+1)
. When
\bar{\delta}_{N}\geq c_{2}(\iota_{N}^{m}+1)
, it follows that for any
\tau\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big\{[h_{j}^{\ddagger}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0,(j)}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)]^{2}\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}<\iota_{N})\mathds{1}((\|\eta\|_{\infty}+\|Y\|_{\infty})<\gamma_{1}^{-1}\iota_{N})\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left[c_{3}(\iota_{N}^{m}+1)(S_{1}S_{2})^{-2/(d_{x}+d_{y}+1)}\right]^{2}.

Let
f^{\ddagger}(x,y,\tau)=(h_{1}^{\ddagger}(x,y,\tau),\dots,h_{d_{y}}^{\ddagger}(x,y,\tau))^{\top}
. It is straightforward to verify that
f^{\ddagger}
can be implemented by a ReLU network in
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN},\Lambda}^{d_{y}}
with depth
L=L^{*}+3
, width
M=d_{y}M^{*}
and Lipschitz constant no more than
2c_{2}d_{y}(d_{x}+d_{y}+1)(\iota_{N}^{m}+1)
. Hence, we have for any
\tau\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big\{\|f^{\ddagger}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}<\iota_{N})\mathds{1}((\|\eta\|_{\infty}+\|Y\|_{\infty})<\gamma_{1}^{-1}\iota_{N})\Big\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{d_{y}}\mathbb{E}_{P}\Big\{[h_{j}^{\ddagger}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0,(j)}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)]^{2}\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}<\iota_{N})\mathds{1}((\|\eta\|_{\infty}+\|Y\|_{\infty})<\gamma_{1}^{-1}\iota_{N})\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle d_{y}\left[c_{3}(\iota_{N}^{m}+1)(S_{1}S_{2})^{-2/(d_{x}+d_{y}+1)}\right]^{2},

provided that
\bar{\delta}_{N}\geq c_{2}(\iota_{N}^{m}+1)
and
\Lambda_{N}\geq 2c_{2}d_{y}(d_{x}+d_{y}+1)(\iota_{N}^{m}+1)
. Recall that
\bar{\delta}_{N}=(\log N)^{(1+\kappa)/2}
,
\Lambda_{N}=(\log N)^{(1+\kappa)/2}
and set
\iota_{N}
to
[2\varsigma^{-1/2}\gamma_{1}^{-1}(\log N)^{1/2}]\vee 1
. We conclude that for sufficiently large
N
satisfying
\bar{\delta}_{N}\geq c_{2}(\iota_{N}^{m}+1)
,
\Lambda_{N}\geq 2c_{2}d_{y}(d_{x}+d_{y}+1)(\iota_{N}^{m}+1)
and
\log N\geq(\varsigma\gamma_{1}^{2}/4)\vee 1
, it holds that

\displaystyle\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN},\Lambda}^{d_{y}}}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}_{P}\|f(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\inf_{f\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN},\Lambda}^{d_{y}}}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big\{\|f(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N})\mathds{1}((\|\eta\|_{\infty}+\|Y\|_{\infty})\leq\gamma_{1}^{-1}\iota_{N})\big\}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle+c_{1}(\bar{\delta}_{N}^{2}+1)\exp(-\varsigma\gamma_{1}^{-2}\iota_{N}^{2}/4)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}_{P}\big\{\|f^{\ddagger}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0}(X,Y_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\mathds{1}(\|X\|_{\infty}\leq\iota_{N})\mathds{1}((\|\eta\|_{\infty}+\|Y\|_{\infty})\leq\gamma_{1}^{-1}\iota_{N})\big\}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle+c_{1}(\bar{\delta}_{N}^{2}+1)\exp(-\varsigma\gamma_{1}^{-2}\iota_{N}^{2}/4)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{4}\left\{\left[(S_{1}S_{2})^{-2/(d_{x}+d_{y}+1)}(\log N)^{m/2}\right]^{2}+\frac{(\log N)^{1+\kappa}}{N}\right\},

where
c_{4}
is a constant not depending on
S_{1},S_{2}
and
N
. This completes the proof.
∎

To commence, Lemma
B.10
, Lemma
B.11
and Lemma
B.12
indicate

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\|\hat{v}^{s}_{N}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{1}SL\log S(\log N)^{3+2\kappa}}{N}+c_{2}\left\{\left[(S_{1}S_{2})^{-2/(d_{x}+d_{y}+1)}(\log N)^{m/2}\right]^{2}+\frac{(\log N)^{1+\kappa}}{N}\right\},

where
c_{1},c_{2}
are constants not depending on
S,L,S_{1},S_{2}
and
N
, and
S_{1},S_{2}
satisfy the conditions that the network depth
L\leq c_{3}(d_{x}+d_{y}+1)^{2}S_{1}\log S_{1}+3
, network width
M\leq c_{4}2^{d_{x}+d_{y}+1}d_{y}(d_{x}+d_{y}+1)S_{2}\log S_{2}
for some universal constants
c_{3}
and
c_{4}
, when
N
is sufficiently large and
N\geq\mathrm{Pdim}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{NN}})
. Therefore, by letting
S_{1}=\mathcal{O}(N^{(d_{x}+d_{y}+1)/(2(d_{x}+d_{y}+1)+4)})
and
S_{2}=\mathcal{O}(1)
, we obtain

M=\mathcal{O}(1),\quad L=\mathcal{O}\left(N^{\frac{(d_{x}+d_{y}+1)}{2(d_{x}+d_{y}+1)+4}}\log N\right),\quad S=\mathcal{O}(M^{2}L)=\mathcal{O}\left(N^{\frac{(d_{x}+d_{y}+1)}{2(d_{x}+d_{y}+1)+4}}\log N\right),

yielding

\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\|\hat{v}^{s}_{N}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)-v_{0}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau\leq c_{5}N^{-\frac{2}{d_{x}+d_{y}+3}}(\log N)^{6+2\kappa},

where
c_{5}
is a constant not depending on
N
, for
N\geq 2
.

Next, we proceed to tackle the conditional density estimation error. Fix any
x\in\mathcal{X}^{s}
. Consider the following two ODEs

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}Z_{\tau}

\displaystyle=v_{0}(x,Z_{\tau},\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau,\quad Z_{0}\sim N(0,I_{d_{y}}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\hat{Z}_{\tau}

\displaystyle=\hat{v}^{s}_{N}(x,\hat{Z}_{\tau},\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau,\quad\hat{Z}_{0}\sim N(0,I_{d_{y}}).

We denote the particles at time
\tau\in[0,1]
as
Z_{\tau}(x,z)
and
\hat{Z}_{\tau}(x,z)
given
Z_{0}=z
and
\hat{Z}_{0}=z
, respectively. Note that

W_{2}^{2}(\rho_{0,x}\|\hat{\rho}_{x}^{s})\leq\int\|Z_{1}(x,z)-\hat{Z}_{1}(x,z)\|_{2}^{2}\lambda(z)\mathrm{d}z,

where
\lambda(\cdot)
represents the density function of the
d_{y}
-dimensional standard Gaussian distribution. For
\tau\in[0,1]
, define

H_{\tau}(x)=\int\|Z_{\tau}(x,z)-\hat{Z}_{\tau}(x,z)\|_{2}^{2}\lambda(z)\mathrm{d}z.

Then, it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}H_{\tau}(x)=

\displaystyle 2\int\left\langle v_{0}(x,Z_{\tau}(x,z),\tau)-\hat{v}^{s}_{N}(x,\hat{Z}_{\tau}(x,z),\tau),Z_{\tau}(x,z)-\hat{Z}_{\tau}(x,z)\right\rangle\lambda(z)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\int\left\langle v_{0}(x,Z_{\tau}(x,z),\tau)-\hat{v}^{s}_{N}(x,Z_{\tau}(x,z),\tau),Z_{\tau}(x,z)-\hat{Z}_{\tau}(x,z)\right\rangle\lambda(z)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle+2\int\left\langle\hat{v}^{s}_{N}(x,Z_{\tau}(x,z),\tau)-\hat{v}^{s}_{N}(x,\hat{Z}_{\tau}(x,z),\tau),Z_{\tau}(x,z)-\hat{Z}_{\tau}(x,z)\right\rangle\lambda(z)\mathrm{d}z.

Specifically, we first observe that

\displaystyle 2\int\left\langle v_{0}(x,Z_{\tau}(x,z),\tau)-\hat{v}^{s}_{N}(x,Z_{\tau}(x,z),\tau),Z_{\tau}(x,z)-\hat{Z}_{\tau}(x,z)\right\rangle\lambda(z)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int\|v_{0}(x,Z_{\tau}(x,z),\tau)-\hat{v}^{s}_{N}(x,Z_{\tau}(x,z),\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\lambda(z)\mathrm{d}z+H_{\tau}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Y^{s}_{\tau}|X^{s}=x}\|v_{0}(x,Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)-\hat{v}^{s}_{N}(x,Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}+H_{\tau}(x).

In addition, the Lipschitz continuity of
\hat{v}^{s}_{N}
and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality suggest that

\displaystyle 2\int\left\langle\hat{v}^{s}_{N}(x,Z_{\tau}(x,z),\tau)-\hat{v}^{s}_{N}(x,\hat{Z}_{\tau}(x,z),\tau),Z_{\tau}(x,z)-\hat{Z}_{\tau}(x,z)\right\rangle\lambda(z)\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\Lambda_{N}H_{\tau}(x).

Hence, we conclude that

\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}H_{\tau}(x)\leq(1+2\Lambda_{N})H_{\tau}(x)+\mathbb{E}_{Y^{s}_{\tau}|X^{s}=x}\|v_{0}(x,Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)-\hat{v}^{s}_{N}(x,Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}.

By Lemma
A.7
, we have

W_{2}^{2}(\rho_{0,x}\|\hat{\rho}_{x}^{s})=H_{1}(x)\leq\exp(1+2\Lambda_{N})\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}_{Y^{s}_{\tau}|X^{s}=x}\|v_{0}(x,Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)-\hat{v}^{s}_{N}(x,Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau.

Therefore, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{s}=\mathbb{E}\left[W_{2}^{2}(\rho_{0,X^{s}}\|\hat{\rho}^{s}_{X^{s}})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\exp(1+2\Lambda_{N})\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{E}\|v_{0}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)-\hat{v}^{s}_{N}(X^{s},Y^{s}_{\tau},\tau)\|_{2}^{2}\mathrm{d}\tau

\displaystyle\leq c_{5}N^{-\frac{2}{d_{x}+d_{y}+3}}(\log N)^{6+2\kappa}\exp\left(1+2(\log N)^{(1+\kappa)/2}\right),

for
N\geq 2
. Furthermore, by Lemma 4.6, we conclude that

\mathcal{E}^{t}\leq c_{6}N^{-\frac{2}{d_{x}+d_{y}+3}}(\log N)^{7+2\kappa}\exp\left(1+2(\log N)^{(1+\kappa)/2}\right),

where
c_{6}
is a constant not depending on
N
, for
N\geq 2
. This completes the proof.
∎

C
Proof of Theorem
A.6

Lemma C.1
(Lemma 11.2 in
[
19
]
).

Let
V_{1},\dots,V_{n}
be independent and identically distributed random variables,
0\leq V_{i}\leq B
,
0<\alpha<1
, and
\nu>0
. Then,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i}-\mathbb{E}(V_{1})|}{\nu+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i}+\mathbb{E}(V_{1})}>\alpha\right\}\leq\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i}-\mathbb{E}(V_{1})|}{\nu+\mathbb{E}(V_{1})}>\alpha\right\}<\frac{B}{4\alpha^{2}\nu n}.

(7)

Remark 7
.

If we substitute
B
, the upper bound of
V_{i}
, to
B_{n}
varying with
n
, the right-hand side of Eqn. (
7
) should be modified to
B_{n}/(4\alpha^{2}\nu n)
and the proof stays the same.

Lemma C.2
.

Let
Z,Z_{1},\dots,Z_{n}
be independent and identically distributed random vectors with dimensions of
d
, and let
\mathcal{H}
be a set of nonrandom functions
h:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to[0,A_{n}]
, where
A_{n}>0
is a nonrandom sequence. Assume
\alpha>0
,
0<\epsilon<1
. Then, for
n\geq 1
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left\{\sup_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\frac{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i})-\mathbb{E}[h(Z)]}{\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i})+\mathbb{E}[h(Z)]}>\epsilon\right\}\leq 4\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\alpha\epsilon}{5},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\mathcal{H}_{|\mathcal{D}_{n}}\right)\right]\exp\left(-\frac{3\epsilon^{2}\alpha n}{40A_{n}}\right),

(8)

where
\mathcal{D}_{n}=\{Z_{1},\dots,Z_{n}\}
.

Remark 8
.

Lemma
C.2
is a generalization of Theorem 11.6 in
[
19
]
.

The proof contains four steps.

Step 1. Substitution for the expectation by an empirical mean. Draw a pseudo-sample
\mathcal{D}_{n}^{\prime}=\{Z_{1}^{\prime},\dots,Z_{n}^{\prime}\}
as an independent copy of
\mathcal{D}_{n}
. Let
h^{*}\in\mathcal{G}
be a function satisfying

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h^{*}(Z_{i})-\mathbb{E}[h^{*}(Z)]>\epsilon\left\{\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h^{*}(Z_{i})+\mathbb{E}[h^{*}(Z)]\right\},

if there exists any such function; otherwise, let
h^{*}
be an arbitrary element of
\mathcal{H}
. We note that the conditions

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i})-\mathbb{E}[h(Z)]>\epsilon\left\{\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i})+\mathbb{E}[h(Z)]\right\},

and

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i}^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}[h(Z)]\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4}\left\{\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i}^{\prime})+\mathbb{E}[h(Z)]\right\},

imply

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i}^{\prime})>\frac{3\epsilon\alpha}{4}+\frac{\epsilon}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i})-\frac{\epsilon}{4n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i}^{\prime})+\frac{3\epsilon}{4}\mathbb{E}[h(Z)],

which is equivalent to

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{5\epsilon}{8}\right)\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i}^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle\frac{3\epsilon}{8}\left(2\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i}^{\prime})\right)+\frac{3\epsilon}{4}\mathbb{E}[h(Z)].

We further obtain that

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i}^{\prime})>\frac{3\epsilon}{8}\left(2\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i}^{\prime})\right),

since
0<1-5\epsilon/8<1
and
\mathbb{E}[h(Z)]\geq 0
. As a result, it then follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists h\in\mathcal{H}:\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i}^{\prime})>\frac{3\epsilon}{8}\left(2\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i}^{\prime})\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h^{*}(Z_{i})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h^{*}(Z_{i}^{\prime})>\frac{3\epsilon}{8}\left(2\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h^{*}(Z_{i})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h^{*}(Z_{i}^{\prime})\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h^{*}(Z_{i})-\mathbb{E}[h^{*}(Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]>\epsilon\left\{\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h^{*}(Z_{i})+\mathbb{E}[h^{*}(Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]\right\},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h^{*}(Z_{i}^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}[h^{*}(Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4}\left\{\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h^{*}(Z_{i}^{\prime})+\mathbb{E}[h^{*}(Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]\right\}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg\{\mathds{1}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h^{*}(Z_{i})-\mathbb{E}[h^{*}(Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]>\epsilon\left\{\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h^{*}(Z_{i})+\mathbb{E}[h^{*}(Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]\right\}\right),

\displaystyle\times\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h^{*}(Z_{i}^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}[h^{*}(Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]\leq\frac{\epsilon}{4}\left\{\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h^{*}(Z_{i}^{\prime})+\mathbb{E}[h^{*}(Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]\right\}\Bigg|\mathcal{D}_{n}\right)\Bigg\}.

Lemma
C.1
yields that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h^{*}(Z_{i}^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}[h^{*}(Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]>\frac{\epsilon}{4}\left\{\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h^{*}(Z_{i}^{\prime})+\mathbb{E}[h^{*}(Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]\right\}\Bigg|\mathcal{D}_{n}\right)

(9)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\frac{A_{n}}{4(\epsilon/4)^{2}\alpha n}=\frac{4A_{n}}{\epsilon^{2}\alpha n}.

Therefore, for
n>8A_{n}/(\epsilon^{2}\alpha)
, the probability in Eqn. (
9
) is no less than
1/2
, and we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists h\in\mathcal{H}:\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i}^{\prime})>\frac{3\epsilon}{8}\left(2\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i}^{\prime})\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h^{*}(Z_{i})-\mathbb{E}[h^{*}(Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]>\epsilon\left\{\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h^{*}(Z_{i})+\mathbb{E}[h^{*}(Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}\left(\exists h:\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i})-\mathbb{E}[h(Z)]>\epsilon\left\{\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i})+\mathbb{E}[h(Z)]\right\}\right).

This proves

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\exists h:\frac{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i})-\mathbb{E}[h(Z)]}{\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i})+\mathbb{E}[h(Z)]}>\epsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists h\in\mathcal{H}:\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i}^{\prime})>\frac{3\epsilon}{8}\left(2\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i}^{\prime})\right)\right\},

when
n>8A_{n}/(\epsilon^{2}\alpha)
. For
n\leq 8A_{n}/(\epsilon^{2}\alpha)
, on the other hand, the right-hand side of Eqn. (
8
) exceeds one, and hence the assertion holds true trivially.

Step 2. Introduction of Rademacher random variables. Let
U_{1},\dots,U_{n}
be independent Rademacher random variables which are uniformly distributed over
\{-1,1\}
, meanwhile independent of
\mathcal{D}_{n}\cup\mathcal{D}_{n}^{\prime}
. Importantly, note that
\mathcal{D}_{n}
and
\mathcal{D}_{n}^{\prime}
are interchangeable with respect to corresponding components while their joint distribution remains invariant. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists h\in\mathcal{H}:\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i}^{\prime})>\frac{3\epsilon}{8}\left(2\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i}^{\prime})\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists h\in\mathcal{H}:\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}[h(Z_{i})-h(Z_{i}^{\prime})]>\frac{3\epsilon}{8}\left(2\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[h(Z_{i})+h(Z_{i}^{\prime})]\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists h\in\mathcal{H}:\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}h(Z_{i})>\frac{3\epsilon}{8}\left(\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i})\right)\right\}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists h\in\mathcal{H}:\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}h(Z_{i}^{\prime})<-\frac{3\epsilon}{8}\left(\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i}^{\prime})\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists h\in\mathcal{H}:\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}h(Z_{i})>\frac{3\epsilon}{8}\left(\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(Z_{i})\right)\right\}.

Here, we note that
-U_{i}
is identically distributed as
U_{i}
.

Step 3. Conditioning and covering. Given
Z_{i}=z_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,n
, and consider

\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists h\in\mathcal{H}:\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}h(z_{i})>\frac{3\epsilon}{8}\left(\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(z_{i})\right)\right\}.

Let
\delta>0
and let
\mathcal{C}_{\delta}
be a
\delta
-covering set of
\mathcal{H}
constrained on
\{z_{1},\dots,z_{n}\}
with respect to the supremum norm. For any
h\in\mathcal{H}
, there exists a vector
h^{\sharp}=(\bar{h}(z_{1}),\dots,\bar{h}(z_{n}))^{\top}\in\mathcal{C}_{\delta}
, such that
\max_{i=1,\dots,n}|h(z_{i})-\bar{h}(z_{i})|<\epsilon
, thereby indicating

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}h(z_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}\bar{h}(z_{i})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}[h(z_{i})-\bar{h}(z_{i})]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}\bar{h}(z_{i})+\max_{i=1,\dots,n}|h(z_{i})-\bar{h}(z_{i})|\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}\bar{h}(z_{i})+\delta,

and

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(z_{i})\geq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{h}(z_{i})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|h(z_{i})-\bar{h}(z_{i})|\geq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{h}(z_{i})-\delta.

As a result, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists h\in\mathcal{H}:\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}h(z_{i})>\frac{3\epsilon}{8}\left(\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(z_{i})\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists h^{\sharp}\in\mathcal{C}_{\delta}:\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}\bar{h}(z_{i})+\delta>\frac{3\epsilon}{8}\left(\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{h}(z_{i})-\delta\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\mathcal{C}_{\delta}|\max_{h^{\sharp}\in\mathcal{C}_{\delta}}\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}\bar{h}(z_{i})>\frac{3\epsilon\alpha}{8}-\frac{3\epsilon\delta}{8}-\delta+\frac{3\epsilon}{8n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{h}(z_{i})\right\}.

Specifying
\delta=\epsilon\alpha/5
deduces that

\frac{3\epsilon\alpha}{8}-\frac{3\epsilon\delta}{8}-\delta=\frac{3\epsilon\alpha}{8}-\frac{3\epsilon\alpha}{40}-\frac{\epsilon\alpha}{5}=\frac{\epsilon\alpha}{10}.

By choosing
\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon\alpha/5}
as an
\epsilon\alpha/5
-covering set of minimal size, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists h\in\mathcal{H}:\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}h(z_{i})>\frac{3\epsilon}{8}\left(\alpha+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(z_{i})\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\epsilon\alpha}{5},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\mathcal{H}_{|\{z_{1},\dots,z_{n}\}}\right)\max_{h^{\sharp}\in\mathcal{C}_{\epsilon\alpha/5}}\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}\bar{h}(z_{i})>\frac{\epsilon\alpha}{10}+\frac{3\epsilon}{8n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{h}(z_{i})\right\}.

Step 4. Leveraging the Hoeffding’s inequality. Note that given fixed
z_{1},\dots,z_{n}
,
U_{1}\bar{h}(z_{1}),\dots,U_{n}\bar{h}(z_{n})
are independent random variables with mean zero and absolute bound
\bar{h}(z_{1}),\dots,\bar{h}(z_{n})
(recall that
\bar{h}\in[0,A_{n}]
). Therefore, Hoeffding’s inequality suggests that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}\bar{h}(z_{i})>\frac{\epsilon\alpha}{10}+\frac{3\epsilon}{8n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{h}(z_{i})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left\{-\frac{2n^{2}[\frac{\epsilon\alpha}{10}+\frac{3\epsilon}{8n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{h}(z_{i})]^{2}}{4\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{h}(z_{i})^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left\{-\frac{2n^{2}[\frac{\epsilon\alpha}{10}+\frac{3\epsilon}{8n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{h}(z_{i})]^{2}}{4A_{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{h}(z_{i})}\right\}

\displaystyle=\exp\left\{-\frac{9\epsilon^{2}}{128A_{n}}\frac{[\frac{4\alpha}{15}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{h}(z_{i})]^{2}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{h}(z_{i})}\right\}.

Note that for any
a,y>0
,
(a+y)^{2}/y\geq 4a
, implying

\frac{[\frac{4\alpha n}{15}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{h}(z_{i})]^{2}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{h}(z_{i})}\geq\frac{16\alpha n}{15}.

Hence, it concludes that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}\bar{h}(z_{i})>\frac{\epsilon\alpha}{10}+\frac{3\epsilon}{8n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{h}(z_{i})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left\{-\frac{9\epsilon^{2}}{128A_{n}}\frac{[\frac{4\alpha n}{15}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{h}(z_{i})]^{2}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{h}(z_{i})}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left\{-\frac{3\epsilon^{2}\alpha n}{40A_{n}}\right\},

which completes the proof.
∎

The proof is composed of six steps.

Step 1. Symmetrization. We commence by replacing
\mathbb{E}[g(f,Z)]
through an empirical mean deduced by a pseudo-sample
\mathcal{D}_{n}^{\prime}=\{Z_{1}^{\prime},\dots,Z_{n}^{\prime}\}
independent of
\mathcal{D}_{n}
. Consider a function
f^{*}\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
depending on
\mathcal{D}_{n}
, such that

\mathbb{E}[g(f^{*},Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f^{*},Z_{i})\geq\epsilon(\alpha+\beta)+\epsilon\mathbb{E}[g(f^{*},Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}],

if such a function exists; otherwise, we let
f^{*}
be an arbitrary element in
\mathcal{F}_{n}
. Then, Chebyshev’s inequality implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\mathbb{E}[g(f^{*},Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f^{*},Z_{i}^{\prime})>\frac{\epsilon}{2}(\alpha+\beta)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}[g(f^{*},Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]\Bigg|\mathcal{D}_{n}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{Var}[g(f^{*},Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]}{n\{\frac{\epsilon}{2}(\alpha+\beta)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}[g(f^{*},Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]\}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\zeta_{n}\mathbb{E}[g(f^{*},Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]}{n\{\frac{\epsilon}{2}(\alpha+\beta)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}[g(f^{*},Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]\}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\zeta_{n}}{\epsilon^{2}(\alpha+\beta)n},

where the last inequality stems from
x/(a+x)^{2}\leq 1/(4a)
for
x\geq 0
and
a>0
. Thus, for
n>8\zeta_{n}/[\epsilon^{2}(\alpha+\beta)]
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left\{\mathbb{E}[g(f^{*},Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f^{*},Z_{i}^{\prime})\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}(\alpha+\beta)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}[g(f^{*},Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]\Bigg|\mathcal{D}_{n}\right\}>\frac{7}{8},

yielding that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}:\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i}^{\prime})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i})\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}(\alpha+\beta)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}[g(f,Z)]\right\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f^{*},Z_{i}^{\prime})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f^{*},Z_{i})\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}(\alpha+\beta)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}[g(f^{*},Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]\right\}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\mathbb{E}[g(f^{*},Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f^{*},Z_{i})\geq\epsilon(\alpha+\beta)+\epsilon\mathbb{E}[g(f^{*},Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[g(f^{*},Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f^{*},Z_{i}^{\prime})\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}(\alpha+\beta)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}[g(f^{*},Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg(\mathds{1}\left(\mathbb{E}[g(f^{*},Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f^{*},Z_{i})\geq\epsilon(\alpha+\beta)+\epsilon\mathbb{E}[g(f^{*},Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]\right)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\mathbb{E}[g(f^{*},Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f^{*},Z_{i}^{\prime})\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}(\alpha+\beta)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}[g(f^{*},Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]\right\}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{7}{8}\mathbb{P}\left\{\mathbb{E}[g(f^{*},Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f^{*},Z_{i})\geq\epsilon(\alpha+\beta)+\epsilon\mathbb{E}[g(f^{*},Z)|\mathcal{D}_{n}]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{7}{8}\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}:\mathbb{E}[g(f,Z)]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i})\geq\epsilon(\alpha+\beta)+\epsilon\mathbb{E}[g(f,Z)]\right\}.

To conclude, for
n>8\zeta_{n}/[\epsilon^{2}(\alpha+\beta)]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}:\mathbb{E}[g(f,Z)]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i})\geq\epsilon(\alpha+\beta)+\epsilon\mathbb{E}[g(f,Z)]\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{8}{7}\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}:\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i}^{\prime})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i})\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}(\alpha+\beta)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}[g(f,Z)]\right\}.

Step 2. Randomization for
\mathbb{E}[g(f,Z)]
. By introducing additional conditions, we notice that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}:\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i}^{\prime})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i})\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}(\alpha+\beta)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}[g(f,Z)]\right\}

(10)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\exists f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}:\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i}^{\prime})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i})\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}(\alpha+\beta)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}[g(f,Z)],

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i})^{2}-\mathbb{E}[g(f,Z)^{2}]\leq\epsilon\left\{\alpha+\beta+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i})^{2}+\mathbb{E}[g(f,Z)^{2}]\right\}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i}^{\prime})^{2}-\mathbb{E}[g(f,Z)^{2}]\leq\epsilon\left\{\alpha+\beta+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i}^{\prime})^{2}+\mathbb{E}[g(f,Z)^{2}]\right\}\Bigg)

\displaystyle+2\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}:\frac{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i})^{2}-\mathbb{E}[g(f,Z)^{2}]}{\alpha+\beta+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i})^{2}+\mathbb{E}[g(f,Z)^{2}]}>\epsilon\right\}.

Then, Lemma
C.2
verifies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}:\frac{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i})^{2}-\mathbb{E}[g(f,Z)^{2}]}{\alpha+\beta+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i})^{2}+\mathbb{E}[g(f,Z)^{2}]}>\epsilon\right\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{(\alpha+\beta)\epsilon}{5},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\{g(f,\cdot):\mathcal{Z}\to\mathbb{R},f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}\}_{|\mathcal{D}_{n}}\right)\right]\exp\left(-\frac{3\epsilon^{2}(\alpha+\beta)n}{40\xi_{n}^{2}}\right).

Next, we focus on the first probability on the right-hand side of Eqn. (
10
). The second inequality inside the probability demonstrates that

(1+\epsilon)\mathbb{E}[g(f,Z)^{2}]\geq(1-\epsilon)\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i})^{2}-\epsilon(\alpha+\beta),

which equals to

\frac{1}{2\zeta_{n}}\mathbb{E}[g(f,Z)^{2}]\geq\frac{1-\epsilon}{2\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i})^{2}-\frac{\epsilon(\alpha+\beta)}{2\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)},

while the third inequality is processed in the same manner. By the assumption that
\mathbb{E}[g(f,Z)^{2}]\leq\zeta_{n}\mathbb{E}[g(f,Z)]
for all
f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}
, the first probability on the right-hand side of Eqn. (
10
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\exists f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}:\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i}^{\prime})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i})\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}(\alpha+\beta)

\displaystyle+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\left[\frac{1-\epsilon}{2\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i})^{2}-\frac{\epsilon(\alpha+\beta)}{2\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)}+\frac{1-\epsilon}{2\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i}^{\prime})^{2}-\frac{\epsilon(\alpha+\beta)}{2\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)}\right]\Bigg\}.

This shows

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\exists f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}:\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i}^{\prime})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i})\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}(\alpha+\beta)+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{E}[g(f,Z)]\right\}

(11)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\exists f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}:\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[g(f,Z_{i}^{\prime})-g(f,Z_{i})]\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}(\alpha+\beta)-\frac{\epsilon^{2}(\alpha+\beta)}{2\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)}

\displaystyle+\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{4\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[g(f,Z_{i})^{2}+g(f,Z_{i}^{\prime})^{2}\right]\Bigg\}

\displaystyle+8\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{(\alpha+\beta)\epsilon}{5},\|\cdot\|_{\infty},\{g(f,\cdot):\mathcal{Z}\to\mathbb{R},f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}\}_{|\mathcal{D}_{n}}\right)\right]\exp\left(-\frac{3\epsilon^{2}(\alpha+\beta)n}{40\xi_{n}^{2}}\right).

Step 3. Introduction of Rademacher random variables. Let
U_{1},\dots,U_{n}
be independent Rademacher random variables which are uniformly distributed over
\{-1,1\}
, meanwhile independent of
\mathcal{D}_{n}\cup\mathcal{D}_{n}^{\prime}
. We note that
\mathcal{D}_{n}
and
\mathcal{D}_{n}^{\prime}
are interchangeable with respect to corresponding components while their joint distribution remains invariant. As a consequence, the first probability on the right-hand side of Eqn. (
11
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\exists f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}:\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}[g(f,Z_{i}^{\prime})-g(f,Z_{i})]\geq\frac{\epsilon}{2}(\alpha+\beta)-\frac{\epsilon^{2}(\alpha+\beta)}{2\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)}

\displaystyle+\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{4\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[g(f,Z_{i})^{2}+g(f,Z_{i}^{\prime})^{2}\right]\Bigg\},

which is further bounded by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\exists f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}:\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}g(f,Z_{i}^{\prime})\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4}(\alpha+\beta)-\frac{\epsilon^{2}(\alpha+\beta)}{4\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)}+\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{4\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i}^{\prime})^{2}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\exists f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}:\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}g(f,Z_{i})\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4}(\alpha+\beta)-\frac{\epsilon^{2}(\alpha+\beta)}{4\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)}+\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{4\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i})^{2}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\exists f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}:\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}g(f,Z_{i})\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4}(\alpha+\beta)-\frac{\epsilon^{2}(\alpha+\beta)}{4\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)}+\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{4\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,Z_{i})^{2}\Bigg\}.

Step 4. Conditioning and Covering. Given
Z_{i}=z_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,n
, consider

\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\exists f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}:\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}g(f,z_{i})\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4}(\alpha+\beta)-\frac{\epsilon^{2}(\alpha+\beta)}{4\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)}+\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{4\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,z_{i})^{2}\Bigg\}.

Let
\delta>0
and let
\mathcal{C}_{\delta}
be a
\delta
-covering set of
\{g(f,\cdot):\mathcal{Z}\to\mathbb{R},f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}\}
constrained on
\{z_{1},\dots,z_{n}\}
with respect to the supremum norm. For any
f\in\mathcal{F}
, there exists a vector
h^{\sharp}=(h(z_{1}),\dots,h(z_{n}))^{\top}\in\mathcal{C}_{\delta}
, such that
\max_{i=1,\dots,n}|g(f,z_{i})-h(z_{i})|<\epsilon
, thereby indicating

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}g(f,z_{i})\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}h(z_{i})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}\left[g(f,z_{i})-h(z_{i})\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}h(z_{i})\right|+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|g(f,z_{i})-h(z_{i})|

\displaystyle<\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}h(z_{i})\right|+\delta,

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,z_{i})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(z_{i})^{2}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[g(f,z_{i})^{2}-h(z_{i})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(z_{i})^{2}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[g(f,z_{i})-h(z_{i})\right]\left[g(f,z_{i})+h(z_{i})\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(z_{i})^{2}-\frac{2\xi_{n}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|g(f,z_{i})-h(z_{i})|

\displaystyle>\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(z_{i})^{2}-2\delta\xi_{n}.

Hence, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\exists f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}:\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}g(f,z_{i})\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4}(\alpha+\beta)-\frac{\epsilon^{2}(\alpha+\beta)}{4\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)}+\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{4\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,z_{i})^{2}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\exists h^{\sharp}\in\mathcal{C}_{\delta}:\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}h(z_{i})\right|+\delta\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4}(\alpha+\beta)-\frac{\epsilon^{2}(\alpha+\beta)}{4\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)}

\displaystyle+\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{4\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(z_{i})^{2}-2\delta\xi_{n}\right]\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\mathcal{C}_{\delta}|\max_{h^{\sharp}\in\mathcal{C}_{\delta}}\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}h(z_{i})\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4}(\alpha+\beta)-\frac{\epsilon^{2}(\alpha+\beta)}{4\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)}-\delta-\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)\delta\xi_{n}}{2\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)}

\displaystyle+\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{4\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(z_{i})^{2}\Bigg\}.

Next we set
\delta=(6\eta-2)\epsilon\beta/(30\eta+3\gamma\eta)=\varpi\epsilon\beta
. Then, when
n\geq N
, we have
\zeta_{n}\geq\eta
,
\xi_{n}\leq\gamma\zeta_{n}
, and for
0<\epsilon\leq 1/2
,

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon\beta}{4}-\frac{\epsilon^{2}\beta}{4\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)}-\delta-\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)\delta\xi_{n}}{2\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)}

\displaystyle=\epsilon\beta\left(\frac{1}{4}-\frac{\epsilon}{4\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)}-\varpi-\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)\varpi\xi_{n}}{2\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\epsilon\beta\left(\frac{1}{4}-\frac{1}{12\zeta_{n}}-\varpi-\frac{\varpi\xi_{n}}{10\zeta_{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\epsilon\beta\left(\frac{1}{4}-\frac{1}{12\eta}-\varpi-\frac{\varpi\gamma}{10}\right)\geq 0.

Therefore, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\exists f\in\mathcal{F}_{n}:\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}g(f,z_{i})\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon}{4}(\alpha+\beta)-\frac{\epsilon^{2}(\alpha+\beta)}{4\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)}+\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{4\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g(f,z_{i})^{2}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\mathcal{C}_{\varpi\epsilon\beta}|\max_{h^{\sharp}\in\mathcal{C}_{\varpi\epsilon\beta}}\mathbb{P}\Bigg\{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}U_{i}h(z_{i})\right|\geq\frac{\epsilon\alpha}{4}-\frac{\epsilon^{2}\alpha}{4\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)}+\frac{\epsilon(1-\epsilon)}{4\zeta_{n}(1+\epsilon)n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h(z_{i})^{2}\Bigg\}.
